ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u1	The meeting was resumed. REPORT OF THE DEFENDER OF THE PEOPLE RESPONSIBLE FOR MANAGEMENT DURING THE YEAR 2014: - REPORT OF THE PEOPLE'S DEFENSE CORRESPONDING TO THE MANAGEMENT DURING THE YEAR 2014. [[File number 260/000005]]
ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u2	The first item on the agenda is the report of the Ombudsman on the management carried out during 2014. I would like to say here, as President of Congress, that I congratulate and congratulate the Ombudsman and the whole body for the speed they have had in carrying out this report and that it can be discussed in depth in the Commission and seen in this House in the first quarter of the year. The Ombudsman, Mrs Soledad Becerril, has the floor to present this report.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u3	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, on behalf of the Ombudsman's institution, I wish to express before this House the deep feeling of pain and sorrow that we all have in the institution, because of the accident that occurred last Tuesday in the French Alps, with so many victims, among them certainly fifty-one Spaniards. We greatly appreciate and recognize the work, assistance and support that the Government of Germany, the Government of France and, of course, the Government of Spain are giving. Mr President, as your honourable Member said, we are presenting this report for 2014 as quickly as possible so that your ladies and gentlemen can - they have already had the opportunity in the Commission - study it and make the assessments and assessments they have considered most timely and close in time. The objective or, rather, one of the objectives of the institution's 2014 financial year has been to achieve greater effectiveness in the fulfilment of our objectives, to have a greater capacity to respond and to respond more quickly to the complaints or demands that citizens have sent us over these months; to facilitate the citizens' approach to the Ombudsman through various means and instruments, computers and the more traditional ones as well; to do so so so that people with disabilities can also find the means with ease to approach the Ombudsman, and to achieve greater speed in the answers, both ours and those of the administrations. Transparency - you know it very well because you have participated in the elaboration and processing of the law - for us consists not only in giving the data of our activity, the salaries, the contracts, the travel, the why of the trips, what is worth maintaining the institution day by day; we already did so even before the entry into force of the Law of Transparency, we already had that page of transparency now completed, but for us transparency goes a little further and consists in giving the citizen the information that he needs or who asks us and giving it to us in a clear way, in a simple way and as quickly as possible. That is why we have introduced on our website a portal in which we can check the average times of what it takes us to respond to the citizen, but also the average times of what it takes all the administrations to respond, which takes the city council of a capital or a small municipality, which takes a council of a community or what takes a ministry of the government of the nation to respond. This will force us all because we all come out reflected with our activity and the times on the website. The number of complaints in 2014 has been almost equal - somewhat higher - to the year 2013 with 23,186 complaints. All this documentation is very sketchy, and you have seen it in the Commission where we have had the opportunity to discuss it extensively, and it basically refers to four paragraphs. In the economic field, access to minimum income is of great concern. The problems of persons with some degree of dependence in the assessment of that unit after recognition and in the provision are also of concern. There are some matters of great concern within the field of taxes - ladies and gentlemen - but there is one thing that is particularly worrying within this field that is the taxes on real property. I am sure that your ladies and gentlemen can transfer this concern to the extent that land classified as urbanizable due to crises and difficulties has not been urbanized, but it is declared and pays IBI as urbanizable, although it will not be developed now or possibly in the coming years. We have many complaints that raise this problem of what these citizens are doing paying those taxes for something that is not a reality, that has not become what they were expected to be. Your ladies and gentlemen told us in the Commission that we should strengthen our actions on our own initiative, problems which we see as being diagnosed and which encourage us to make a small report and address it to the administrations. That's how we did it. In 2013 we had 347 ex officio proceedings and in 2014 we had 487. There has been an increase in work and initiative. On what matters? In the area of education, for example, the inclusion of pupils with disabilities, an issue of great concern in schools and of great concern to families who are truly distressed by the situation of whether the child is not fully integrated into a school like all others. In the treatment of cancer diseases, you know very well the problems of medicines, and in liver diseases because it is a situation of concern and anguish for thousands and thousands of people. We have also acted in relation to ground clauses, clauses in many abusive cases because the signatory of the corresponding loan did not know what the ground clauses were and could not imagine that in two years he would get out of hand and that these clauses would cost him the loan that way. We've acted, too. The recommendations, which are a classic mode of action of the Ombudsman and very important, have also increased and have been 467 this year. The most important of these has been housing. We have managed to maintain in the tax reform the reduction coefficients - with a certain limitation, it is true -, which allow for the day a person goes to sell a house that he bought in the 1990s not to spread his updated price, because there is a reduction coefficient, as his name indicates. We have also managed - it has been during the last few days, but it was done in 2014 - to expand the Social Housing Fund so that the largest number of families can benefit. Your ladies and gentlemen are well aware of housing problems and know that, in addition to being important, the autonomous communities and the municipalities have a great deal of work to do in this respect, because the housing parks that have not been able to sell over the last few years, for the reasons we all know, can be made available to the citizens by means of moderate rents, acceptable to a large sector of the same. We have recommended this, and I would like to remind you, ladies and gentlemen, that you are doing well in your autonomous communities and districts to reiterate the reasonable provision of the floors which, above all, large municipalities had planned to sell and it has not been and will not be possible to sell in the coming times. Our recommendations on child feeding, especially during the holiday and summer periods, I consider modestly that they have been interesting and important. They didn't like everyone, it's true, because some of the people responsible told us that we were discriminating. I have already said that the greatest discrimination for a child is not having a meal, as he has had the rest of the year at the school. When the holidays arrive, the schools have to organize as they see fit - we do not get involved - how they will continue to give such food to the children, whether it is in a sports camp or with other classes or complementary activities, because closing two months the school eateries for many families and children is not only a problem, but a deficiency. I told the President at the beginning of my speech that I am going to be very brief and that I am going to make just one summary, because I know that your ladies and gentlemen have a lot of work to do today, but before I finish I want to highlight two actions which, in part - I am not just saying, but in part - come from the Ombudsman and which have or may have a significant significance. I understand that one of them will be discussing it in the immediate future and I am referring to the law known as the second chance law - its name is already very indicative - which is in the process. They will remember, especially the people who are in the Joint Commission, how we discussed in 2013 a report by the Ombudsman called economic crisis and mortgage debtors. At the end of this report, a second chance law was recommended and it was called this because that name is very similar to that of other countries. This law exists in several countries of the European Union, in the vast majority, and in the United States; as your ladies and gentlemen will see in your debate, there is an absolutely new, important, unusual way, which did not exist in the modern and contemporary history of Spain, a way for those people and families who have been indebted under different circumstances - we cannot analyse them at this time - to see a light at the end, so that through a judicial or extra-judicial agreement with a mediator, also a notary way, without cost, they can arrive at a postponement, a withdrawal, to pay that debt or part of it by giving ownership or something of the small assets that they can have and be released forever from debt and can start again. It's something similar to what can happen to a company, but now it can happen to a family and a private individual. We attach great importance to this opening road, which is to be opened in the immediate future and, although it is possible that in the future this law will have to be modified and adapted to different circumstances, it is absolutely unusual and we are very happy, because, as I said at the beginning, it is a way of solution. There was no such possibility and, if it did not exist, it could lead to the submerged economy because any work a person could get in this situation would mean that his salary or income would be automatically retained and then he would have no incentive to look for a job. However, it is now going to have a way out within a reasonable time, and that seems very important to us and we are very happy about it. The second question concerns the extension of the so-called code of good practice for banking institutions - it is a code to which they voluntarily adhere, but there are all banking institutions - and it is extended on occasion - you will see at the time - of the Decree-Law of 27 February. The evictions are stopped until 2017, an absolutely fundamental issue; the ground clauses are eliminated in certain circumstances, often abusive clauses, and the negotiation of debts with banks is allowed: deadlines, cancellations, payment methods. It was a necessary enlargement and our assessment is positive. Among the studies we have carried out over this time, I will highlight two. One is related to childhood and child protection. This is not just about hearing the child, but about hearing it in family proceedings. We have done so with prosecutors and experts not only from the institution, but from outside it: psychologists and others. We have also made allegations or comments on the family-friendly project, the so-called Child Protection Act, highlighting the importance of family-friendly care. It is much better, in our view, for minors to be in family care than in a centre, even if they are well maintained and cared for. Children are much better in a home, in a family. These considerations were brought to the ministry at the time when it was drafting the Child Protection Bill. The other report or document prepared in collaboration with the autonomous defenders is the so-called: Hospital emergencies in the National Health System. We did it in 2014, although it was already completed in 2015. We find it interesting because it is a general analysis of the system, although not hospital by hospital since we do not have that capacity. Over the course of two years we have collaborated with a working group of hospitals and autonomous communities, plus autonomous defenders, and we have an opinion of how the emergencies are working, the appreciation that the Spanish have for these services and their importance. It seems to be a lie, but in 2013 there were 26 million entries for these services. This does not mean that all these people will remain in emergency rooms, but there were 26 million entries for these services. We have dealt primarily with two things: the staff who have - whether it is enough or not - and the right to privacy, privacy and information of patients who come in accompanied or not by their relatives in hospital emergencies, which falls within our competence. As you know, by recognition of these General Courts and at the request of the United Nations, we have the capacity to be a national mechanism for the prevention of torture and ill-treatment. Therefore, we frequently visit detention centres for foreigners. In Spain we visited in 2014 sixty-one centers accompanied by experts from outside the institution: psychiatrists, psychologists and forensic doctors. I myself visited twelve detention centres in Spain in 2014 and three detention centres in European Union countries - in the cities of London, Paris and Rotterdam - in order to observe their functioning and whether these visits could lead to any recommendation to improve the conditions of ours. I have sent the Minister of the Interior recommendations, above all, on the maintenance of centres and infrastructure and on how inmates can relate to the consulate, lawyers or NGOs, fundamentally Red Cross or some similar. There have been some comparisons that can be interesting and improve our centers. Also part of our ordinary work, although not as a preventive mechanism, is the visit to the temporary stay centers that are mainly in Ceuta and Melilla. They are well-maintained centres by staff, but they are overly occupied. Your ladies and gentlemen know, you have dealt with it and I am not going to go back to the matter, but you are overly busy and it is very difficult to maintain the best conditions for the daily life of these people, families and minors who are in temporary residence centres. I will now conclude, Mr President, by telling your ladies and gentlemen that we understand the difficulties that administrations, municipalities and local authorities, public bodies, councils or the government of the nation may have in responding. Sometimes they can say: Again the institution of the Ombudsman asks us to expand, again says that the information is not complete, etc. We understand the difficulties, especially because there are administrations that do not have much capacity to devote people to these things, and we understand it, but I remind you - as far as your ladies and gentlemen are concerned - that it is not the Ombudsman who complains, that it is not a thing invented or imagined by the Ombudsman, but that behind a complaint there is a person, and what we do is to transfer those circumstances to the administrations. The complaint is in the history of the peoples since ancient times, but now in some plays that are currently on display we hear the complaint again. The complaint is in Calderon, the complaint is in Shakespeare, there was already talked about the complaint; in Life is Dream says Calderon: if your complaint is reasonable, I will attend to your complaint. It is in all literature, it is not an invention of the institution of the Ombudsman. I understand the difficulties, but we cannot abandon our obligations, and we have to pass on these complaints and manage to solve these problems. Sometimes we notice that there is a distancing or a disaffection - to put it milder - with respect to the people we hold a public office, but there may be reasons for it. If the complaint exists, I repeat, we have to take care of it. I think it is better that we correct our mistakes, that we review our criteria, because this social and democratic state, as reflected in our Constitution, and which I am sure your ladies and gentlemen are proud of, is the one that allows reforms; there are other political systems that do not allow any reform. Mr President, I thank you very much for your attention to all your ladies and gentlemen and to the Commission itself, which has dealt very much with these issues, and I conclude by reminding you - I am sure you know this - of the report of the last quarter of the Bank of Spain, because it has some connotations or makes a hopeful analysis of the coming times. I do not want to end only with the complaints, I also want to say that if Spain follows a certain path - which will be discussed and constantly discussed in this house - thanks to the efforts of all the Spaniards, we can hope for a better future, a more prosperous future, a future to get out of the crisis. From the institution of the Ombudsman I hope that we can give positive answers and solve many of the problems that come to that house every day. Thank you very much, Mr President. [[Applauds]]
ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u4	Thank you very much, Mrs Becerril, for your presence and for your words. Let us begin, in turn of fixing positions, the interventions of the groups. Firstly, for the Joint Group, Mrs Pérez Fernández has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u5	Thank you, Mr. President. The Ombudsman has presented us here today the report for 2014, a report that, as has been said, is very similar quantitatively to 2013, due to the number of citizens' complaints. We particularly appreciate the improvements in transparency in the work of the institution, as well as the increase in ex officio proceedings and the number of recommendations made by the Ombudsman. It was precisely in the presentation in this House of the 2013 report that the Ombudsman told us that the report showed a part of the reality. Well, the first thing we have to say is that it is clear that the part of the reality shown in the institution's document coincides, and very much, with the reality and concerns that we have moved over the past year by the opposition parliamentary groups; a reality that differs greatly from the idyllic vision that the Government and the parliamentary group that supports it have repeatedly drawn up for us, and that shows, as we have said many times, that the policies that are being implemented, especially in the social sphere, are being devastating for the majority of the population. The 2014 report clearly distinguishes three main areas - we do not do exactly the same division as the Ombudsman did, but it is very similar - which are the ones that raise the most complaints and those that clearly reflect the situation that millions of people are suffering in the Spanish State, and on the other hand, who really favour anti-social policies in our opinion that are being carried out: the economic sphere, which highlights above all the continuing abuses of banking entities; the social sphere, with all that it involves and covers, and where a greater number of complaints are registered in this year of 2014, and the field of security and justice. Everything concerning the ministries responsible for these two fundamental policies of every rule of law presents a desolating picture. These three broad features clearly indicate the general picture, where the abuse of banking entities and their lack of respect for existing legislation effectively predominates, and even because of the fact that court rulings, such as the elimination of the ground mortgage clauses, are handed down. It is a picture where it is shown once again that the legislation drawn up and put into effect on mortgage debtors is not only insufficient, but has proved to be useless so far. It was useless at the time, as well as throughout this time, to reveal the code of good practice, which is still a declaration of intent. The Mortgage Act adopted in 2013, among other things, has not only not prevented the continued growth of the number of dwellings with which banks are left, but has also encouraged the reduction of mortgage-to-entity agreements and payments. The second chance law, in process, in which the Ombudsman herself seems to be putting a lot of hope, is also not going to be the definitive solution to a situation that keeps the rate of mortgage executions exactly the same as five years ago, almost one hundred and twenty a day, a truly shocking figure that has not yet made the Government react, eight years after the start of the crisis. Finally, everything relating to housing - the Ombudsman has expressed this in her speech, and the complaints and recommendations contained in her report - indicate that much remains to be done in this area, and that what has been done has not been done well. In the economic sphere too, although it obviously affects the social sphere - everything has its social implications - we would like to refer briefly to the abuses of the large electricity companies. It is an important chapter of the Ombudsman's report, which already occupied a large part of the previous year's report. The abuse of electricity finally translates into one - not the only - of the causes of the increase in energy poverty in the Spanish State; more than four million people in this situation.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u6	Mrs. Pérez, you have to finish.

Child nutrition and child poverty are also the subject of evaluation and criticism - although in our view very mild - by the Ombudsman; education and health, areas where complaints have increased, precisely in line with those we have made by the groups. Today, with the final adoption of the law on citizen security, we will have a new proof that it is going backwards in areas of fundamental rights, which cover the field of justice and security. We understand that much remains to be done, and we would like to agree with the Ombudsman in her assessment of the improvement of the data of the Banco de España, but the reality of the street and that which is given through complaints to the Ombudsman indicates something else very different. Thank you very much, Mr President.

Then, not signing an agreement allowing, for example, the Greuges Syndic to visit the detention centre for foreigners in Barcelona, which is what needs to be done, goes against the Convention against Torture. You, Ombudswoman of the People, have appealed against the Catalan law regulating the Synod of Greuges, but - how curious! - you have not appealed against the Spanish law that buried universal jurisdiction. Look, you have little concern that the murderers are unpunished, that the fascists, the hierarchs of Francoism, who are still here, do not have to account for their abuses, or everything that had formed this will of Spanish society to advance in the persecution of the genocidals throughout the world, that here was buried with the new law that ended with universal jurisdiction, and that you did not appeal, as we do not believe that you are going to appeal the new Penal Code or the Mordaza Law. ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u12	Thank you very much, Mr. Tardà. The Ombudsman is not present in the Chamber. It is in the rostrums, but not in the Chamber, as is the custom from the beginning. For the Basque Parliamentary Group, Mr Olabarría has the floor. [[PNV]]
ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u13	Thank you, Mr. President. Madam Ombudsman, I again welcome your presence in this House. I would like to take these first words to congratulate Ararteko, whose term of office ends today. It's been brilliant management. I think you have had a relevant level of collaboration. Mr Lamarca has done well, he has exercised the functions of counterpower or alternatives to power that correspond to an institution such as you represent from a neutral perspective, neutrality that with all honesty I have to tell you that it is not always perceived in your memories or in some more valuable aspects of your memories, although it must be acknowledged that you are making an effort to address the most relevant problems and the most visible democratic deficits of the Spanish State. Here we find ourselves with several problems. It is the first memory in which you do not make manifestly ideological reflections. I remember that in the memoir that was presented in 2013 you glossed over the Parot doctrine or defended its maintenance, even having already fallen back from the Court of Strasbourg declaring it illegal. He also reflected on how the Parot doctrine should be applied. These are not functions for which you are legitimized. On the other hand, we have also not heard him say anything about what is happening with ombudsmen of the people of the autonomous communities. Here is a recurring problem at the connection points. We do not know how far they go and what the powers of the defenders of the autonomous people and those of the Ombudsman of the Spanish State should be. There is no kind of point of connection, as there is with the laws, which determines the scope for which they are entitled to act. But without prejudice to this, what is happening is something more serious. Autonomous ombudsmen are disappearing. I have not heard any reflection on whether you are concerned or correct about this action emanating from the Law on the Reform of Public Administrations, in the final analysis of the CORA's reflections to avoid duplication, and it seems that all the duplications that remain, according to what is being empirically observed, are of an autonomous nature. There is no duplicity on the state level that seems to be left. There are five defenders of the people who have disappeared or are in the trance of disappearing, and they are barely kept - I don't know if this situation is going to be maintained - those who have the greatest historical projection, the greatest historical tradition, even greater than the institution you represent, such as the Ararteko, the Syndic of Greuges and the Justice of Aragon. Every time a reform linked to the well-deserved CORA is carried out or committed, the autonomous state structure is bitten. Then we will have the opportunity to gloss over, in a paradigmatic example, the Civil Protection Act, the amendments of which are to be discussed at a later stage. I believe that you have a moral and ethical obligation, I do not know whether legal or statutory, to reflect on this which is taking place in the context of the autonomous fact, of the autonomous processes, and not only of the elimination of institutions and structures in their entirety of the autonomous sphere, but of the degradation of competences through different procedures that arise, either from the usurpation of the legislative function committed in this legislature by the abuse of the real decree-law as a natural and almost unique way of legislative action, or from the perspective that each rule, every structural reform that is subject to approval, involves a rescue and reduction of autonomous competences, even those already transferred, not of the pending transfers. From that perspective we would like some reflection - these would be ideological reflections, but for which you are legitimized, as long as they refer to the configuration of the constitutional bloc - that we always miss in your reports. In this report, which we are examining today, we see a more diffused ideological profile. You cannot dispense with your ideology, and your team is plural as well, as there are people who have different political sensibilities. I imagine that they will act reasonably collegiately and will be able to make elements of synthesis, but I do not just understand - because you never mention it, and you have already appeared on several occasions in this House - what are the areas of action of the autonomous ombudsmen and what are the areas of the ombudsmen's ombudsmen you represent. You cannot reasonably intervene in matters which are located in the autonomic sphere, but you could intervene in matters of supraautonomic, state-level. But this is not so, there is a kind of permanent interconnection and penetration into improper or alien skills. When you intervene or demand from some autonomous communities your intervention should perhaps be referred back to the Ombudsman of that autonomous community, if it exists, and vice versa, when it is a problem of a supraautonomous nature obviously the defenders of the people, when they are denounced this situation, should in turn be referred back to you. These points of connection, this sort of ordination between the competences of each other, are in some way disturbing the functioning of these institutions, which are so important at a time when the system of fundamental rights and public freedoms, enshrined in the Spanish Constitution itself, is seriously declining, law after law. Today we are going to have three opportunities to analyse how it is declining, through the debates of the amendments that come from the Senate of two important rules: a part of the Criminal Code relating to terrorist offences, which has been broken down - eccentricity wherever it is - from the Criminal Code itself, and the law on citizen security, on which I am very surprised that you have not been able to perceive that the law on citizen security, through an over-dimension of the concept of public order and an absolute denaturization of the concept of citizen security, is limiting the essential content of the practice of all the fundamental rights enshrined between articles 14 to 29 of the Constitution. As for the issues that you are dealing with through recommendations your performance may be weak or labile - if I may express it and I do not want to be offensive. Even the administration of justice is more belligerent than the institution you represent in certain areas that you have cited. With regard to the ground clauses and to the preferentialists already in the Administration of Justice 90 % of the claims referred to the prefernts are being declared frauds or scams, criminal figures with criminal relevance and which also deserve not only the reparation of those affected by these financial machinations, but that even those sanctions or penalties which may correspond to real criminal injustices are being imposed; criminal injustices which are also - as in so many other things in this context - subject to far more belligerent action than theirs by the Administration of Justice. In fact, a curious phenomenon is taking place. The Administration of Justice, which has never enjoyed in the Spanish State the statistics of a particularly relevant appreciation by citizens, is becoming the true structure of counterpower through the emergence of causes of corruption and the denunciation of mortgage law, where you have made an almost apologetic mention of the law of the second chance. It's just that you have to go further and you know it, because this does not help to solve a problem that is located in the Mortgage Law and the Civil Code. The fact that it has already been declared - in this case by the Court of Luxembourg, the Court of the European Union - illegal is the maintenance of the mortgage debtors of the debt alive for them, concerning or compromising all their present or future assets. Reflections of this nature and how to correct them is certainly what citizens expect from a much more belligerent action than that which you are glossing, and only by making apologetic reflections of some legal instruments that do not even come to what the judgment of the Court of Luxembourg on Spanish mortgage law has decided: that it is the illegal practice of this unlimited conversion, of this conversion into adaptations to the debt of citizens who have had the misfortune of not being able to face a debt of a mortgage character. It has also referred to emergency services and the over-dimension of hospital emergency services. How will hospital emergency services not be oversized when, after recent reforms of our health system, for foreigners in an irregular situation is the only procedure for access to public health? Of course, but denounce the cause, do not denounce the effect or the inadequacy of the means in the hospital system. He complained that it was for this reason, that it was the only system of access for foreigners in an irregular situation to access health care. It comes from something dramatic. A country with dignity, which meets the requirements of article 9 of the Constitution and the guarantee and driving effect of public institutions in order to eliminate inequality among citizens, must at least end - if it is considered democratic and dignified - hunger, particularly hunger in the child sphere. I am pleased with your invocations regarding the need to keep the school dining rooms open through any procedures so that at least one child can have a decent or reasonable meal a day. But look at the drama of the situation that comes from what we're talking about. He also spoke of non-compliance with the Unit Act. In other words, a good Unit Law is made, but it is not funded because the responsibility for its implementation lies largely with the Autonomous Communities and the situation of the different Autonomous Communities from the financial point of view and from their political decisions is most heterogeneous, in practice and in truth the Unit Law becomes a cause of frustration, because in many Autonomous Communities it simply does not apply or apply in such a scarred and delayed way that many of the people who aspire to benefit from that Law actually do not receive the minimal attention that the Unit Law proposes as a virtually basic right for the persons concerned. I am glad that you have insisted so much on this issue in your presentation because the problem is extremely serious. It is extremely serious that Spain is a country that has risen to the highest levels of poverty within the European Union, above other countries - and I always say this, I think we must remember this because it indicates the terrible policy of managing the crisis - that is to say that we have more child poverty than countries that have had to be rescued whole, not only its banking system as the case of Spain, which was also rescued, but also as Greece or Portugal, indicates an absence of social policy, a failure of the institutions and a missharing of competences truly tragic on many occasions for the people who suffer it and of course for the future of the country, as we have also said many times. Here I have not heard at least express denials from the Government regarding the existence of the problem of poverty, but only an absolutely insufficient and ineffective response. Of course, they end up using the Ombudsman. And here there is another paradox that says a lot about our state's failures. As I have seen in the report, most of the complaints to the National Ombudsman are the result of misdeeds by local authorities. It is normal, because the problems on which these autonomous administrations are competent are precisely those that cause the complaints, that is, education, dependence, health, housing, etc. But that's finally what happens, people are citizens of a country where the autonomous administrations have competences that are well exercised, poorly assumed or poorly financed, and in the end the pagan, who is frustrated in his legitimate aspirations to live in a decent state, I say no more in a Nordic welfare state, but in a decent state, finds that no one gives him any answer and this ends here. And that is a very serious defence of the citizens, because we are once again talking about a special service such as the Administration of Justice but which is also a power of the State. Then we have a power of the State abandoned by a decision that I do not think is innocent but that I believe it is deliberate, that it is a policy of weakening the administration of justice precisely to strengthen the power of the executives to make decisions that in practice cannot be corrected by the counterpowers and counterweights of a well-organized state of law. This is what is happening constantly, this is what we see in the background of the memory, an abandoned Justice Administration that cannot therefore perform its functions and that was further worsened in its capacity to respond by the crazy judicial fees that still circulate and run around and that have been reduced but not completely eliminated because they still affect nothing less than SMEs, to set an example. I do not think it is the job of the Ombudsman's institution to be glad that the Government finally recognizes that it has done absolutely nothing and that it is now going to do something. I think it has to be somewhat more critical than just a transmission mechanism and that it distributes parabens or recommendations or complaints to the different institutions. The result is that the Ombudsman, who is an institution that should act much more punctually and much more aimed at correcting specific failures or abuses or motivated complaints, becomes a sort of great showcase of what is the underlying problem, which is the failure of democratic institutions in Spain. I cannot overlook in any way an unfair ruling of the Constitutional Court of 5 March 2015. In 2010, the previous Ombudsman, Mr Enrique Múgica, filed an appeal for unconstitutionality because he considered that the Law of the Parliament of Catalonia, which regulates the institution of the Synod of Greuges, invaded the competences of the State. In particular, and in the first place, the powers of torture and other cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment assigned to the Registrar of Greuges are not within his competence. In 2006, Spain corrected the optional protocol to the Convention against Torture and Other Cruel, Inhuman or Degrading Treatment or Punishment, and as the Constitutional Court considers that the protocol is part of the field of international relations, whose competence is State, it therefore considers that it is the competence of the institution of the Ombudsman and not of the Syndic of Greuges. The sentence limits the functions of the Greuges Syndic to being able to conduct interviews or to require the opinion of the doctor; and I add: it would be missing more! That is the misermal interpretation that the Constitutional Court makes with a restrictive reading of article 149.1.3 of the Constitution, and obvious what is actually established in article 17 of the Protocol itself and its spirit, and more so when it speaks of delegation to decentralized entities. Secondly, the territorial competence of the Greuges syndic with regard to the powers of the Ombudsman. The Constitutional Court merely invokes the judgment given in respect of the Catalonian Autonomy Statute of 2010, but adds that, once Article 78 of the Statute has been clarified, the supervision of the Greuges Syndic on the local administration and the bodies associated with it is not exclusive, so it does not exclude that of the Ombudsman, since it is competent to supervise any public administration, whether state, autonomous or local. Curiously, in no case does the Constitutional Court's ruling speak of establishing agreements between the Greuges Syndic and the Ombudsman, nor does it speak of the principle of subsidiarity between the Greuges Syndic and the Ombudsman, nor of the principle of proximity in favour of the Greuges Syndic, nor does it invoke the principle of autonomy. Curious. But it is more curious that the Constitutional Court, on its third basis, appeals to the principle of voluntarity in reference to the inclusion of the principle of cooperation between article 78 of the Statute and...

Various measures have been taken in this legislature in recognition and support of the victims of terrorism. In order to preserve the memory of the victims and build the story of the truth, the Memorial Centre for Victims of Terrorism has been established in Vitoria, which has a national character and whose objectives are to preserve and disseminate the democratic and ethical values embodied in the victims, to build memory, to delegitimize terrorism and to raise awareness among the population as a whole for the defence of freedom, human rights and against terrorism. As always the People's Parliamentary Group has been, is and will always be with the victims, who constitute for this party a genuine ethical and moral reference in the struggle for freedom and democracy. Complaints include those related to delay in the area of justice. As you stated, the workload and the tools available to the Administration of Justice could be the possible causes. The current electronic systems used do not facilitate the work, but the opposite. For this reason, the Ministry of Justice has presented the timetable of measures with which it intends to reach the goal of a paperless justice in January 2016. The introduction of new technologies in the Administration of Justice will put in place a battery of measures that could lead to the definitive modernisation of justice; that in early 2016 the role of the courts will disappear will be a step that will become a reality, establishing the obligation of electronic communication between legal groups and courts. Another measure that will be introduced in the coming months will be the new electronic judicial headquarters, through which any person involved in a judicial process will be able to know how their proceedings go with the justice system. We are also informed in your report by complaints from 857 citizens, who express to the Ombudsman their dissatisfaction with specific judicial decisions. The complaints raise concerns, in this case, about the family processes in which children are affected and which have led the Ombudsman to decide to draw up the study called Listening and the best interests of the child. As I have already indicated, the General Courts are in the process of processing the draft Organic Law amending the system for the protection of children and adolescents. This reform, which includes recommendations from the Ombudsman, amends up to twelve laws, including the Organic Law 1/1996 of 15 January on the legal protection of minors. One of the main objectives is to have a new framework of rights and duties and special attention to the most vulnerable children. For the first time, the duties of minors in the family, school and social spheres are laid down. They are no longer regarded as passive subjects and provide greater protection for the most vulnerable children, who are expressly recognized as having the right to health, education and social services. The laws also affect the greater participation of minors, through the right to be heard and heard. Another objective is to define in a state norm situations of risk and helplessness, to expedite the procedures of reception and adoption. The reforms will give priority to solutions to families, as opposed to residential ones. Three types of reception are established: emergency, temporary and permanent. On the other hand, protection centres for children with behavioural problems are regulated for the first time. Open adoption is another novelty, so that the child in adoption has the opportunity to maintain some kind of contact with members of his or her biological family. The third objective is to strengthen the fight against violence against children, as for the first time children are recognized as victims of gender-based violence. Protection against crimes such as sexual abuse will be strengthened. Of the recommendations made to the Ministry of Health regarding the contribution of users to the co-payment of medicines, the Ministry, as well as eliminating co-payments of transport and others, will require the autonomous communities to abolish the co-payment of hospitals. In this way, patients will not be required to contribute anything for the medicines given to them in hospitals. One of the priorities of the People's Party has been to ensure that Spanish people have access to the best possible health care. Thanks to the reforms, approximately one million unemployed people and their families for the first time do not pay for their medicines. With reference to hepatitis C, Spain becomes the first country in the world to guarantee treatment with new medicines and will benefit around 52,000 patients. In social services, the ministry will strengthen comprehensive support for the family and paternity. To this end, measures such as the tax reform have already entered into force, the aid of which has already claimed over 27,000 families during the first fifteen days of operation. As you know, the General State Budgets for 2015 multiply the support for families with children in poverty. However, we are aware that further efforts must be made to help these families. We must remember that Spain was the OECD country in which inequality grew the most between 2007 and 2011, as did poverty that grew at a faster rate than in the European Union. The risk of poverty and social exclusion increased by 13% in the last socialist legislature, five times more than in the European Union, just as child poverty grew at this time three times faster than in the European Union. These data, according to the National Institute of Statistics, show that the two most important indicators used in Europe to measure social inequality - the 80/20 ratio and the Gini coefficient - are reduced for the first time in the last four years. The Government has made structural reforms to ensure greater social protection and quality public services. The main commitment to this goal has been employment, as a fundamental policy against poverty. The priority is the fight against child poverty. In 2014, a €17.4 million fund was allocated to the Autonomous Communities to combat child poverty. We appreciate your statements regarding the correct conduct of security forces and officials in detention centres, which comply with the law and human rights conventions, and share your view that there are accusations against detention centres and officials responding to strategies of the terrorist gang ETA. On the other hand, the amendment to the draft Organic Law of the Criminal Code, which will be discussed today in this Chamber and which will enter into force shortly, will improve the criminal response which expressly provides for the possibility that probation may be used to prevent danger in crimes against gender-based violence, while at the same time improving the regulation of the crime of trafficking in human beings and of forced prostitution. ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u27	Royal Decree-Law 2/2015 of 6 March 2015 adopting urgent measures to repair the damage caused by floods and other effects of storms of rain, snow and wind that occurred in the months of January, February and March 2015. This royal decree-law will be presented, therefore the Minister of the Interior has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u28	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, allow me first of all, and in so far as I was unable to be here in the House at the sitting on Tuesday afternoon because I was presiding over the crisis room set up at El Prat airport in Barcelona on the occasion of the tragic aviation accident, which we all know, to join publicly from the rostrum the expression of solidarity and pain expressed by the President of Congress, as well as the minute of silence that the House kept. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to present to the House on behalf of the Government Royal Decree-Law 2/2015 of 6 March, which takes urgent measures to repair the damage caused by the floods and other effects of rain, snow and wind storms that occurred in January, February and March this year, in accordance with Article 86 of the Constitution. This royal decree-law came into force, as your lordships know, on 7 March, once published in the State Official Gazette. Also, as you know, during the last days of January, much of the month of February and this month of March, successive rain, snow and adverse weather events of wind and sea have affected virtually all of the national territory and have caused, among other effects, flooding and flooding in different watersheds, determining the declaration of alert status at different levels and the activation of the corresponding territorial or special civil protection plans in various autonomous communities. Special mention is made of the floods caused by the growth of the Ebro River during the month of February, the first days of this month of March and also today - yesterday, as we know - which, as I say to you, are particularly affecting the province of Zaragoza, reaching unknown levels in the last twenty years and which are estimated to have drowned about 20,000 hectares of land. [[The lady]] Ladies and gentlemen, since the beginning of the river we are talking about, in particular the Ebro River, more than 500 people have been mobilized to ensure the safety of the population. Specifically, on Monday, 2 March, the number of persons mobilized was 1,163, 450 of them were members of the Emergency Military Unit; 350 transport vehicles, including helicopters, airships, buses, ambulances, etc. were also used. Yesterday, as you know in the province of Zaragoza, the high flow of the Huerva River has forced the preventive evacuation of 75 people from a residence in Santa Fe and about 100 homes in Cadrete. I would like to highlight the effects that the Government's reaction has been proportionate and appropriate to the situation created, being, as it cannot otherwise be, in permanent coordination with the autonomous communities concerned, with their authorities and in particular with those of the Autonomous Community of Aragon, given the seriousness - I insist - of the floods caused by the growth of the Ebro River, in particular waters up from Zaragoza to the town of Novillas, waters down to Pina de Ebro and the reservoir of Mequinenza. Already on 11 February the Vice-President of the Government, all the government delegates of the Autonomous Communities and the Autonomous Cities of Ceuta and Melilla and I had a meeting in Zaragoza. We have already been talking about this issue - I repeat, 11 February - and the Vice-President of the Government announced publicly on that occasion since Zaragoza that the Government was studying the promulgation of a royal decree-law, which I am currently presenting to your ladies and gentlemen for the constitutionally envisaged procedure of eventual validation. Later, when these important avenues of the Ebro River occurred at the end of February and the first days of this month, as well as damage in other autonomous communities - in Navarre, etc. - but we all know that the most outstanding ones, that the most affected area was the province of Zaragoza and in particular the areas I have commented on from the river; then, on the same 1st March, the Secretary of State for the Environment was there. I was also in Zaragoza on 2 March and had the opportunity to meet with the authorities and fly over the whole area to get a better idea of the impact of those avenues, both upstream of Zaragoza and downstream. We were flying over the town of Pina de Ebro because at that time it was being assessed whether or not the evacuation of the population was necessary, although finally fortunately it was not necessary. On 3 March, the Minister of Agriculture, Food and Environment was also visiting the affected areas and meeting with the various officials. Finally, as honourable Members know, the President of Government on the same day as the Council of Ministers approved this Royal Decree-Law on 6 March, was in Aragon with the President of the Government of the Autonomous Community of Aragon and with other authorities to draw up the Royal Decree-Law, having first-hand knowledge of developments. Of course, it has always been in constant contact with the authorities of the Government of Aragon and with the ministers directly concerned and involved, who had also travelled there. I repeat, on the very day that the royal decree-law was passed the president came, as you know, to Aragon, to Zaragoza. I would like to stress that the approval of the Council of Ministers on 6 March of this royal decree-law was made even when it was not yet possible, for obvious reasons, to carry out a definitive estimate of the damage, given the situation in which some of the affected areas were and are still in place. The Council of Ministers approved it precisely in view of the reality of the consequences and the need to restore the normal functioning of public services as soon as possible. As I said, ladies and gentlemen, as regards the justification and necessity of adopting this royal decree-law, the natural disasters to which I have referred have significantly altered the daily lives of citizens and the normal functioning of communications, while at the same time causing great damage to public-owned infrastructure and also to many private-owned infrastructure. Therefore, as I said to you, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, and by virtue of the authorization provided for in Article 86 of the Constitution, the Council of Ministers has adopted this royal decree-law in order to opt for a set of measures aimed at alleviating the damage caused to persons and property, both of public - I repeat - and private - ownership. [[dwellings, garages, commercial and industrial installations and agricultural holdings]] At the time of this royal decree-law the weather forecasts did not rule out that in subsequent weeks similar episodes could be repeated, as unfortunately is being shown. Precisely because of this provision, it is envisaged that the Government may, by royal decree, extend the application of the measures contained in this royal decree-law to other damages that may occur until 31 May of this year, as far as the temporal scope of application of this royal decree-law is concerned. With regard to the geographical scope of application and in the same line mentioned above, given the general allocation of the territory, a closed list of autonomous communities has not been determined, with reference to the Royal Decree-Law, for the determination of the municipal terms and population units to which these measures will specifically apply, to a subsequent order of the Ministry of the Interior. According to the royal decree-law, that ministerial law will be promulgated - we are preparing it as is evident - from March 31st. As for the material content of the rule that I am presenting to you, although it follows the structure of previous ones approved in this same legislature, the Royal Decree-Law 2/2014 of 21 February and the Royal Decree-Law 25/2012 of 7 September, the present Royal Decree-Law includes some new ones that aim to address the singularities, the specificities of emergencies, as I will briefly point out below and in a very particular way - I insist, not exclusive nor exclusive, but in a particular way, as is evident - that affect the province of Zaragoza because of the growth of the Ebro River. As regards the corresponding aid, if we look at it by ministries, starting with the ordinary aid provided by the Ministry of the Interior and regulated by Royal Decree 307/2005, I must distinguish between two types of direct economic aid. First of all, there are those for personal damage, for material damage to homes and goods, on farms and on industrial, commercial, maritime-fishery, tourist and other services. I would like to highlight a special novelty which contains this royal decree-law with respect to previous ones, and which is in our view, as well as that of the Council of Ministers, very useful for the purpose intended. According to the Ministry of the Interior with the Ministry of Agriculture, Food and Environment, the usual reference to the term agricultural establishments as recipients of aid for damage to agricultural holdings has been replaced. This concept of agricultural holdings, as opposed to agricultural establishments, is much broader and, together with the amendments included in Article 2.4 of the Royal Decree-Law, will allow the aid to be extended to essential elements of non-insurable farming, such as the private roads of farms or the loss of arable land that would have been dragged by water, as it literally quotes - you know - the rule that I am presenting to you for eventual validation. It should also be noted that, as in previous royal decrees-laws approved in this legislature, aid to commercial establishments for the payment of up to 7 % of the franchise applicable by the Insurance Compensation Consortium up to a maximum amount of EUR 8,000, which has proved to be effective in similar previous situations. In fact, the only precedent was in 2014, when seventy-seven grants were granted for an amount of approximately EUR 220,000, whereas on previous occasions and with direct application of the Royal Decree of Ordinary Aid, 307/2005, of the Ministry of the Interior, without the modifications of these Royal Decree-Laws, the aid granted to establishments had been very limited precisely because it did not contain this measure that we have now included; I am referring to the aid for the payment of up to 7% of that franchise applied by the Insurance Compensation Consortium. A second novelty of this rule with regard to precedents is that aid to natural or legal persons who have made personal and property benefits, in addition to local corporations, for expenses incurred in dealing with such emergency situations, as is the case that normally affects municipalities and municipalities, is specifically covered by this rule. [[removal of snow or mud, drainage of water or removal of dead animals]] They will be governed by Royal Decree 307/2005 of 18 March, but the requirements of Royal Decree 307/2005 are made more flexible in the following ways in order to make them more accessible. The deadline for submitting applications for any of these aids is extended from one month to two months. All those who consider themselves legitimate to ask for them, compared to the ordinary month, will have by virtue of this royal decree-law a period of two months. On the other hand, the procedure for granting aid requested by the Ministry of the Interior reduces the time limit to be determined from the six months provided for on a regular basis to three months. The time limit for submitting applications for aid is therefore doubled and the time limit for resolving the relevant procedures is reduced by half. The accreditation of ownership of damaged property is also made more flexible, and any document that can prove the ownership of the damaged property is admitted as a means of proof of ownership of real property. All such aid shall be paid from the appropriations provided for in the existing budget of the Ministry of the Interior in the Law on General Budgets of the State under the heading of the Directorate-General for Civil Protection and Emergencies. From January to 25 of this month, that is to say, until yesterday, in all the autonomous communities affected by the various causes to which he previously referred, a total of nine hundred and forty-five petitions have been submitted for processing in the corresponding delegations and sub-delegations of the Government, in the specific area of the competences of the Ministry of the Interior, which are the ones I have pointed out so far. [[rains, snow, winds, storms, avenues, etc.]] If we break them down by autonomous communities, the petitions submitted until yesterday are as follows: ninety-three in Aragon, six hundred and thirty-seven in Castile and León, twenty in Cantabria, fifty-two in the Basque Country, ten in Navarre, one hundred and twelve in La Rioja, eighteen in Andalusia, one in Asturias, one in Catalonia and one in Illes Balears. Ladies and gentlemen, at first glance it may seem that the number of applications submitted to date is not very high, but let me tell you that this should not be missed for the following reasons. Firstly, we have not yet been able to access all the areas affected by the floods - I recalled the situation we experienced yesterday - and, consequently, the damage can be assessed; any assessment so far is merely provisional. Secondly, as I have told you, the deadline for submitting applications established by this royal decree-law is two months from its entry into force. Therefore, as it entered into force on 7 March, there is time until 7 May; I have spoken of those presented until yesterday, 25 March. Finally, as I say to you, the order of the Ministry of the Interior which implements this royal decree-law is still to be approved - it is established that it must be after 31 March, and this will be done immediately - by making concrete the population centres and municipalities to which these aids particularly affect. In addition to the direct economic aid to which I have just briefly referred, the Royal Decree-Law provides for other measures of a very different nature, but all with the common objective - I insist - of trying to restore normality by alleviating the damage caused by these situations. The Directorate-General for Traffic provides for exemption from the fees applicable to the processing of vehicle casualties and the issuing of duplicates of driving or driving licences when requested as a result of such accidents. With regard to other ministries, in particular the Ministry of Agriculture, Food and Environment, in particular because of the characteristics of the damage caused in Aragon, special measures have also been introduced. Thus, this ministry, the Magrama, provides aid for damage caused in agricultural and livestock productions with the following novelty that I want to highlight to your lordships. Additional aid - emphasis on additional aid - is granted to holders of agricultural and livestock holdings who, having in force insurance policies covered by the combined agricultural insurance scheme, have suffered losses exceeding 30 % of their production. In particular, Article 9 of the Royal Decree-Law develops what I have just succinctly expressed to you. Another very special novelty contained in this area in the Royal Decree-Law is that the Council of Ministers may, on a case-by-case basis, and by the corresponding agreement of the Council of Ministers, grant exemption from the environmental impact assessment of works for the repair or rehabilitation of infrastructure, equipment or facilities subject to such assessment, in accordance with Law 21/2013 of 9 December of the Environmental Assessment. The other aids, ladies and gentlemen, which are often included in this type of royal decree-laws and also, as is evident in the present, are those relating to damage to the hydraulic public domain, to the maritime-terrestrial public domain and to rural infrastructure of general use, such as natural roads and green roads. The Ministry of Agriculture, Food and Environment is empowered to declare a special area of action for the restoration of the maritime-terrestrial, hydraulic and general-purpose infrastructure public domain and to declare the emergency of the works to be carried out by that department the aforementioned areas in the royal decree-law. This, of course, is going to speed up much, of course, so that the resulting work can be done to alleviate the damage and, as I say, restore normality as soon as possible. With regard to the activities of the Ministry of Finance and Public Administration in particular, I would point out that, with regard to local infrastructure, up to 50 per cent of its cost of repair is provided for; all repairs or restitution of all types of infrastructure, equipment, facilities and services of municipal ownership, including the ownership of the municipalities, the districts and the road network of provincial and outlying municipalities, the island councils - island councils, we are thinking logically of the Balearic Islands - as well as of the uniprovincial autonomous communities, can be carried out by the emergency procedure. The specification of these local territorial authorities has been at the express request of the Autonomous Communities concerned, because they have a special institutional presence in their territory. On the other hand, ladies and gentlemen, as is normal, tax benefits are also provided for on various taxes, in particular on property tax, with exemption in the quotas for 2015 for housing, industrial and commercial establishments, agricultural holdings, etc. The tax on economic activities shall be reduced in proportion to the time of cessation of activity for those industries, commercial establishments, etc. which have been damaged and which have had to be relocated or temporarily closed down. I insist that this refers to the tax on economic activities. With regard to the income tax on natural persons, aid for personal damage is totally exempt from it. Finally, measures are also envisaged affecting the Ministry of Employment and Social Security, which are, in particular, labour and social security measures. Collective redundancies, suspensions of contracts and reductions in working hours which have a direct effect on damage caused by catastrophic events, as well as loss of activity in the maritime-fishery, hospitality and accommodation sectors, shall be considered as force majeure, so that the employer is exempted from paying social security contributions for the duration of the suspension of the contract on the grounds of such events, and in the event of termination of the contract, redundancies shall be paid by the Guarantee Fund. Companies and self-employed persons shall also obtain a one-year moratorium on the payment of social security contributions for the three calendar months immediately preceding the production of the claim. With regard to the economic impact, which is tentatively estimated, as is evident, the first estimates of total damage to both public and private property are EUR 106 million, of which approximately 100 would correspond to public property and the rest to private property. With regard to public-owned goods - I insist that on a provisional basis - approximately 70% of the shares to be carried out by the Ministry of Agriculture, Food and Environment and 30% of the remaining shares to be carried out by the Ministry of Development. The remaining approximately €6 million that would affect public-private property would be managed - I insist, always on a provisional basis - by the Ministry of the Interior in the field of its competences and would correspond to damage to housing and goods - for the moment we estimate them about half a million euros -, commercial establishments - a provisional estimate about a million euros - and emergency expenses of local corporations -3,5 million euros is the provisional estimate -. All this is without prejudice to the fact that there is a relaxation of the usual requirements for its regular processing, to which I have already referred: twice as much time to submit requests and half as much time to resolve procedures, therefore to respond to those requests. Finally, Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to point out that an interministerial commission is being set up - as, on the other hand, it is customary and necessary - to monitor and implement all the measures contained in this royal decree-law. This inter-ministerial commission shall prepare a report on the activities carried out in implementation of all the forecasts contained in this standard by 30 October of this year. In conclusion, Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, this royal decree-law reflects the Government's unequivocal desire to respond as urgently as possible to the damage caused at the moment between January, February and March, and to those which could eventually occur until 31 May by the aforementioned natural disasters. Ladies and gentlemen, on the basis of what I have just summarised, I request the support of this House for the validation of this Royal Decree-Law 2/2015 within the framework of Article 86 of the Constitution. Thank you very much, Madam President. Thank you very much, ladies and gentlemen. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u33	Thank you, Mr. Salvador. By the same group, Mr. Quadra.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u34	Ladies and gentlemen, while we are discussing this decree-law, there are again floods in Navarre, roadblocks and in some localities the state of pre-alert has been given. These floods are the third of this year, in just three months; the previous ones were very strong and gave rise to this decree-law, but they add up, in turn, to those we had in 2013, in 2007, in 2005, and so on. It has already been said that large rains and floods are inevitable but their effects can be mitigated, and this requires preventive policies and precise plans. It should be recalled here that the European Union in 2007 adopted a mandatory directive for all Member States, which called for the Member States to adopt precise management plans to deal with floods. Eight years have passed and this plan is non-existent in the Spanish State, which is a guilty negligence. It can be said with total certainty that, if these plans had existed, the damage that would have been caused by these floods would have been quite minor. Yesterday, the Foreign Parliament adopted a resolution calling for two things: the first, to make a comprehensive management of the Ebro; and the second, to demand that this plan, which the European Union in its day demanded, be adopted as quickly as possible, because the deadline for doing so is 31 December; eight years of negligence. Rivers are more than just a water channel. They are channels, but they are also banks and flood plains. This directive considers this to be the case, because it understands that the treatment to be given to them in order to anticipate these floods and their damage must, above all, go hand in hand with water, to recover meanders, to recover riparian forests, to reduce the existing moths considerably and to respect the flood plains; not to channel the rivers, but to release them so that the damage is minor in any case. However, today, water, which is a scarce good, has become a business. Large swamps, transfer projects are made; there are also projects to privatize their management and these banks, these flood plains, in turn, are flooded by agricultural farms of all kinds, industrial farms, and are also converted into urban and urban soils. What is needed is to walk in the opposite direction, because if not, what happens and is that from time to time, periodically, rivers descend with their own writings under the arm claiming their domains and then all this damage occurs. Preventive policies are needed; not only aid, which is completely plausible, such as those provided for in this decree-law, but these preventive treatments. With regard to aid, it should be remembered that one thing is to preach and another is to give wheat. In July of last year we had some very strong floods in the north of Navarre, in the Baztan, and there the usual policy was made; rubber boots policy, plumber, easy promises, easy words, but they are still to see the aid that the individuals and the municipal administrations themselves promised then and that they have not arrived. The aid must therefore be greater, rather greater. Agrarian organizations have described those contained in this decree-law as totally inadequate. It is necessary to increase this aid a lot; and it is necessary, above all - because many floods are there knocking on the door - to have a preventive policy, a preventive hydraulic policy. In any case, Amaiur is going to vote in favour of this decree-law because, even if we consider this aid to be very insufficient and critical, it has to reach its beneficiaries as quickly as possible. We demand a substantial increase in these and a hydraulic policy based on the parameters I have commented on.
