ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u77	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, I would like to begin by welcoming and welcoming to this House the entities and associations that accompany us, especially the families of victims of terrorism and victims of crimes that have particularly shocked Spanish society in recent years. Welcome. The plenary session of the Congress is now proceeding to the final adoption of the reform of the Criminal Code, a reform that involves a comprehensive update, attentive to new phenomena and new social demands. From its entry into force on 1 July we will have a better and more agile criminal system, a system that adapts our rules to the international legal framework, that decongests criminal justice by eliminating shortcomings of small entities and that gives criminal response in terms demanded of us by citizens, that is, an effective and proportionate response, to new forms of crime. As has been recalled here, the reform has had a long process with opposition from the opposition, an opposition that insists until the end on the caricature of the reform talking about punitive populism, cuts in freedoms and toughening of penalties, and a caricature that the reform denies with its only reading - something that is evident that some spokesmen today have not done - and that believes the unwillingness to agree that the opposition groups have faced the different parliamentary procedures. It is enough to examine the general part to understand it. In fact, a new penological scheme is introduced, incorporating the permanent prison that can be reviewed as a maximum penalty in the Penal Code, which has - you have no right to ignore or ignore it - with the support of two thirds of the Spaniards, with the endorsement of the Council of State, including illustrious socialists, and which respects Article 3 of the Treaty of Rome and the jurisprudence of the Court of Strasbourg in this matter. It's the same to you. They have placed the emphasis on disqualifying a penalty for crimes of exceptional gravity, the most serious, the most repugnant, the most social alarms produced and the greatest rejection: murders of minors; murders of persons with disabilities; murders with sexual assaults; terrorist murders; and magnicides. You continue to speak of degrading penalties and unconstitutional precepts, and try to disqualify the reform, but it is a reform that does not renounce the reinsertion of the sentenced person and responds to the security that citizens demand. It is a reform that puts the emphasis on justice, because it is fair that more serious conduct leads to more serious convictions. It is a reform that is attentive to citizens and their security, for which much of the speeches that have been heard in this House are incomprehensible. We believe that you are moving away from society with that political stance, a society that had been demanding this punishment for certain behaviors for a long time. Furthermore, we believe that they are moving away from the truth by trying to distort the content of the law. Also in this general part, different legal institutions are reviewed to make criminal justice more flexible, to provide more scope for judges, especially in the execution of minor sentences and, in particular, for perpetrators of crimes committed for the first time. You continue to be framed in your caricature, gentlemen of the opposition, you continue to oppose a modern reform that has tried to avoid automatism and rigidity in the application of criminal law and, above all, in the execution of sentences. After passing through the Senate, articles 48 and 84 abound in this sensitivity. Thus, mental disorder and intellectual disability must be taken into account in the prohibitions on going to or residing in certain places, and the term person with disabilities in need of special protection is adopted in the reform instead of the term incapable. We have defended and continue to defend this reform thinking not of doctrine or academic theory, but of people, in responding to their demands and in solving their problems. It is therefore a reform that emphasizes the protection of persons and property, their rights and freedoms, the protection of minors, the protection of women, a reform to combat corruption and corrupt people in particular. To all this you have opposed and continue to oppose yourselves, with singular ardour the Socialist Party, and listening little, by the way. The passage of the bill by the Senate has made it possible to improve the text submitted by Congress. The reform will make it possible to deal more effectively with repeated crime or multirecidence in crimes against property or property, crimes that have hitherto been considered of little economic importance, in many cases - in others they do - but which generate great disruptions and sensations of profound vulnerability and insecurity to their victims. The EUR 400 limit has been recovered to delimit less serious and minor offences, which provides legal certainty and precision in initiating proceedings, and convictions for minor offences will generate criminal record, which was not the case with the offences, and will therefore be recorded. The criminal type of theft, article 234, includes, as an alleged aggravating factor, the non-use of alarm or security devices and responds proportionately to the serious nature of the act, to the concern generated by thefts or thefts committed on farms or livestock farms or perpetrated by criminal organizations or groups engaged in the commission of crimes of this nature; the perpetrators integrated in them will be subject to a specific aggravating factor provided for both thefts and for the forceful theft of things. The aggravated type of article 242.2 of robbery with violence or intimidation committed in inhabited houses has also been extended to crimes committed in buildings or premises open to the public. This also responds to a clear social demand, especially clear in the case of traders and 1.5 million workers in the commercial sector. With regard to the aggravating of multirecidence, article 250 incorporates the aggravated modalities of the type of fraud, which also apply to offences of unfair administration and misappropriation, as is the case for theft in article 285.7. I do not need to stress, ladies and gentlemen, the social significance of gaining in effectiveness in the criminal struggle in the face of multirecidence in this type of crime. With regard to the special protection of persons, new criminal types are incorporated to prosecute conduct against freedom, dignity and respect for women: types of forced marriage, aggravated in the case of minors; stalking or harassment, defined as an offence against freedom; unauthorized disclosure of recordings or images; and non-use of electronic devices used to control penalties or precautionary and security measures. The new text of article 187 facilitates the persecution of those who exploit the prostitution of another person by enumerating cases in which such exploitation is considered to exist in any case, but without excluding residence from exploitation in cases other than those stated. Similarly to the types of hate and violence offences, which were reinforced to combat and prevent racist or xenophobic behaviour by protecting foreigners and minorities, gender-based discrimination has been incorporated into articles 511 and 512, i.e. discrimination committed by officials, professionals or employers in the performance of their activities. We believe that this is an important element in the fight against all forms of discrimination and, in particular, in the prevention of discriminatory actions that would not be covered by the reference to sex as a basis for them. As I said before, minors will also be better protected after the reform. Internet contacts are established to commit sexual offences or show pornographic material, and pages are planned to be removed or blocked with these content or to be used by persons with disabilities. Finally, in this section on the protection of persons, following the Senate proceedings, article 550 will consider acts of attack against teaching or health officials in the performance of their duties or on their occasion; and those convicted under article 607 for crimes of genocide will also not be able to carry out activities related to the teaching field, as was already envisaged for crimes of terrorism and against sexual freedom. In the fight against corruption, I must stress that the reform as a whole will make it possible to provide a criminal response to the corrupt in the terms demanded by society; that is, by seeing the entry into prison of the corrupt for the performance of their sentences, by removing them from politics and the administration with special disqualification penalties and by achieving the return of the illegitimately acquired. It is a question of protecting society decisively against this criminal phenomenon; and also against the criticism of those who did not walk - and you did nothing - and now say that what is done is insufficient. When you had occasion, you did nothing in this matter. [[Applauds.-A Member: That is what you tell the PP!-Several Members say words that are not perceived.]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u79	Thank you, President. In the fight against corruption, penalties have been increased and criminal rates strengthened. There is a widespread increase in the penalties of special disqualification for employment or public office, as is the case in the crimes of administrative prevarication, infidelity in the custody of documents and the disclosure of secrets, law enforcement, influence trafficking, misappropriation and unfair administration committed by public officials, fraud and illegal sanctions and others. In the most serious crimes, the additional imposition - not alternative, but additional - of another penalty of special disqualification for the exercise of the right to vote is now added. In other words, the person convicted of the offence of corruption is prevented from obtaining a public office by election. This has been done in the crime of conspiracy, in the prevarication of public officials or in the trafficking of influence. And the crime of illegal party financing that so far did not exist has been incorporated. You'll know why you never incorporated him. So far, he didn't exist. In spite of your opposition to this and all of the above, the criminal responsibility of the political parties is maintained, gentlemen of the PSOE. They need to explain why they are opposed to that responsibility. [[Rumors]] The Senate procedure has made it possible to incorporate bribery offences into corruption offences in business. The aim is to criminalize the behaviour of those who pay bribes in order to obtain competitive advantages, both in the private sector and through the corruption of a foreign public official, both for their own benefit and for the benefit of third parties. Finally, with regard to confiscation, a key instrument for this ultimate objective of recovering what corrupt people illegally obtain, two new articles have been incorporated into the Criminal Procedure Act, which are recovered because of their substantive nature for the Criminal Code. It is the articles that regulate the confiscation of property, effects and profits arising from the pre-convicted criminal activity on the basis of relevant indications and certain presumptions. All this, I repeat, to achieve the recovery of the illicitly obtained by the corrupt. I do not want to end without mentioning the reform section dealing with new types of crime. The Senate has improved the accuracy of the types and legal certainty of offences against intellectual property, as does the incorporation of the reference to the risk to the life or health of individuals for offences against public health. The furtivism of shellfish-fishing species has been defined in article 335, and in other areas, such as abandonment and animal abuse, new behaviours are defined and penalties are aggravated, forest fires are also re-treated to increase penalties and introduce aggravated types in response to the behaviours that are most at risk, harm or social alarm. I'm done, ladies and gentlemen. For all this, for this reform, thinking of people, thinking of citizens, we reiterate today the request for support to the House. The opposition, in particular from the PSOE, to a reform that responds to an extensive list of social demands and strengthens the capacity of justice to deal with new forms of crime remains incomprehensible. After year and a half of processing, you continue in caricature, disqualification and not why not. Today, we will support a reform at the service of citizens that responds to what Spanish society demands from its political and criminal justice representatives. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

Then there was a bad start in this area, Minister, but it continued to get worse. Here three operations are committed that are constitutionally doubtful. I do not know whether this law is going to be the subject of the presentation of various remedies of unconstitutionality, but I can almost certainly tell you that it is. First, the dimension given to the concept of citizen security, which is identified with the anachronistic concept of public order. I am not going to use Franco public order as some Members or spokespersons have done but we could be talking about a concept similar to Franco public order. The Spanish Constitution, Minister, Mr Conrado, refers to citizens' security in an instrumental way in two precepts: Article 16 and Article 104. Article 104 does not enshrine fundamental rights, 16 yes, but 16 states that citizen security is instrumental and linked to the exercise of other fundamental rights. As long as this bill elevates the status of citizen security to the character of an ontologically superior right to other fundamental rights, the exercise of any of the rights between articles 14 and 29 of the Constitution, which are those that enshrine fundamental rights, can be strictly discretionaryly restricted by the governing authority. See if the constitutional perversion committed by this project is profound: it raises to the ontological and legal rank citizen security or public order to the point that it is a conditioning element of the exercise of all fundamental rights, which are particularly that of manifestation, freedom of expression and free participation in public affairs. The second perversion that has not been corrected, Minister, but that has worsened in the Senate - I do not know why the Senate has the propensity to worsen the texts emanating from this House -, the second operation that is being committed, I said, is the seizure of public space. Taking over the public space as long as the public space is no longer a space, as constitutionally it is configured, of interaction between citizens from a perspective of tolerance and respect for each other, but that the public space is a space in which citizens can only do what the law allows them to do. This limitation of the nature of public space limited or limited by legal requirements is also a limiting element in the exercise of the fundamental rights provided for in the Spanish Constitution itself, articles 14 to 29. Public space is no longer a space of interaction, it is not a space of relationship, it is not a space of citizens, it is a space of laws where you can only do what the laws allow. Thirdly, the right to effective judicial protection is violated as long as the faults of the Criminal Code are excluded, depriving the defendants, depriving the citizens - I thank you, Minister, for illustrating Mr Conrad so much but I can assure you that Mr Conrad is a very fine lawyer who does not need any particular illustration in this matter - of the guarantees of the criminal process, a process that is much more guaranteed than the contentious-administrative process. From this perspective, we are also faced with a problem of relativization or limitation of the right to effective judicial protection. I do not have time to talk about the conduct that is characterized as administrative offences that refer to the right of demonstration fundamentally. ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u145	Thank you very much, Madam Vice-President. Strengthening democracy is about reforming what works so that it works better, correcting its shortcomings and deviations, improving the quality and functioning of our democratic institutions, and that is exactly what we do with these laws. With the law regulating the exercise of the high office of the General Administration of the State, we establish the requirements of suitability, honorability, experience and professional competence of those who have been called to hold a high office in the General Administration of the State. We guarantee that the exercise of this activity, moreover, is carried out with the maximum conditions of transparency, control of legality and publicity in their statements, reinforcing the Office of Conflicts of Interest. In addition, we reinforce the limitations of the so-called revolving door - let alone say - by running away from populist criteria, which would end up leading little less than to the civilian death of those who have held a position of responsibility in the Government. The limitations on returning to the private activity of the high office must exist, but we understand that this incompatibility should be limited to the two years following its cessation. Rigor yes, ladies and gentlemen, but also proportionality. In any case, this will be a law that will bring a lot to the institutional quality and a lot of transparency to the reality of our senior positions. The second of the laws is the law of control of the economic and financial activity of the parties. A law that affects us all because we have all been elected to lists of parties represented in this House. A law of self-requirement that responds to the principles of exemplaryness, transparency and austerity, which are the principles that must govern the life of the parties in the future and which has been improved with the Senate amendments that we adopted today and that, as has already been said by some of the speakers who have preceded me in the use of the word, will better define some aspects of the law in order to avoid interpretative doubts. It is these principles that justify, among other measures, that debt forgiveness to parties will henceforth be prohibited; that donations from legal persons to parties will be prohibited in order to permanently separate the world from the politics of the business world and that donations from natural persons to parties will also be limited in the amount of 50,000 euros, no more than half of what has been collected so far in the legislation. On the other hand, we will strengthen the mechanisms for monitoring the foundations linked to the parties. It is a law, I said, of self-requirement, but also of austerity and exemplaryness. I know that the adjustment in these years has been very hard and that public funding for the parties has been reduced by no more than 40%. But, ladies and gentlemen, if in these years we have been asking for efforts and sacrifices from citizens it was logical that we, the political parties, were the first to adjust, we were the first to be exemplary. That is why in this law we not only consolidate this 40% reduction in public funding in the parties, but also reduce the mailing subsidy by 20% more and we have linked public funding of the parties in the future, not to the CPI or to the gross domestic product, but to a much fairer parameter, the financing of the parties from now on will be linked to the evolution of social spending in the State's General Budgets. We have also increased accountability with the creation of the figure of the economic and financial head of the parties, and we have severely criminalized the offences in which a party may incur and its corresponding sanctions, which because of their seriousness can reach the qualification of the new type of illegal financing that we have introduced in the reform of the Criminal Code. With regard to the internal functioning of the parties, we have expanded the minimum content of the statutes, which must reflect both the procedure for the election of the governing bodies, management and representation in such a way as to ensure the participation of all participants in such procedures, as well as the procedures for the defence of the rights of the participants and of the sanctioning regime, which will always guarantee the prior hearing of the affiliate, except in two cases: the temporary suspension of the participation of those in criminal proceedings for offences of corruption when an order for the opening of an oral trial has been issued, which will become a final expulsion if after the trial a conviction has been imposed. We do, ladies and gentlemen, because we understand that corruption is incompatible with membership and affiliation to a political party. On this subject and on the basis of what has been said, I would like to reflect. Ladies and gentlemen, corruption always means a breakdown of the civic, ethical and legal principles of every society, but when it has a political nature it also has another component, it means a breakdown of the principle of trust which is the basis of representative democracy. That is why from here we must wage an unbridled struggle against it, open a stage of intransigence against corrupt and irregular behaviour that undermines the credibility of politics and that have done so much damage to confidence in the system and institutions, and that is precisely what we are doing with this reform. Ladies and gentlemen, to strengthen the system and to dignify politics must certainly be to despise and combat corrupt people, but it must also respect the most basic principles of the rule of law. People get corrupted, not acronyms. If we believe in the rule of law, we never extend responsibility beyond who has it, from harm to the guilty, never the innocent. [[Applauds]] Ladies and gentlemen, it is up to us to assess today, of course, and critically, and to legislate; judging and condemning is not up to us. ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u173	Thank you very much, Mr. Castillo.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u174	Discussion of the entire draft law of the National Civil Protection System. For the presentation of the bill, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the Minister of the Interior.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u175	Thank you very much, Mr President. I go to this rostrum to present the draft law of the National Civil Protection System. As you certainly know, in the current law, Law 2/1985 of 21 January on Civil Protection, a first and positive normative framework for civil protection in Spain was established. The current law has been supplemented by numerous subsequent regulatory provisions, some as important as the basic civil protection standard or the basic self-protection standard, while different basic planning guidelines and emergency plans on specific risks have been developed. [[The lady]] In addition, it should be remembered that this rule, the current law of 1985, precedes the current development of our model of autonomous state in the field of civil protection and precedes Spain's accession to the European Union, so I will then briefly comment. It is clear that the evolution of potential risks and the means to be employed, as well as the changes in the approach and organisation of civil protection services, suggest an update of the legal framework in this area, with an emphasis on prevention, as well as on the integration, coordination and efficiency of the actions of the different public administrations in the field of their competences. Ladies and gentlemen, this project aims to strengthen the mechanisms that will enhance and improve the functioning of the National Civil Protection System in the event of emergencies and disasters and, at the same time, to enable better implementation of the commitments made at international level and in the European Union, while updating and ordering the scattered legal framework that we have in this area. There is also a qualitative leap of first order to consolidate the functioning of a national system, respecting, as it cannot otherwise be, the existing jurisdictional delimitation between the State, the autonomous communities and the local entities - it is clear that when I speak of the State I do so within the meaning of article 149 of the Constitution -. For this reason, ladies and gentlemen, we can say that we have passed a law such as the one in force in 1985 - a good law, but that it needs, after thirty years, a thorough updating, a law defining for the first time the civil protection policy in our country - to process a bill that, together with containing for the first time in our country the definition of the civil protection policy, will regulate the appropriate instruments to be able to implement it, instruments that are precisely those that are integrated into the National Civil Protection System. It has been possible to make this qualitative leap because, during the thirty years of the 1985 Act - so far - there has been a great deal of regulatory development in this area, both in the area of state regulations and in the autonomous sphere. At the same time, there has been its own activity in this area in the European Union, as well as new means, new instruments for the management of public policies, such as strategies, without forgetting, of course, the important work of volunteers in this area. Ladies and gentlemen, let me focus briefly on the material content of the project. The draft, in Title I, defines civil protection as described in the case law of the Constitutional Court. Civil protection, as an instrument of public security policy, is the public service that protects people and goods, ensuring an adequate response to the various types of emergencies and disasters caused by natural or human-induced causes, whether accidental or intentional. This is article 1 of the bill, literally - I insist - the jurisprudence of the Constitutional Court, because, as your lords know, there is no express reference in the Constitution to civil protection, which gives us an idea of how society has evolved in this area, and I have also referred to these thirty years between the current law and the current law of 2015. Only article 30.4 of the Constitution provides that, by law, the duties of citizens may be regulated in cases of serious risk, catastrophe or public calamity. For this reason, as we know, it has been the case law of the Constitutional Court that has developed over time the concept and the division of competence in this field, assigning to the State the normative and executive powers with regard to the application of the states of alarm, exception and site, the supraautonomic scope of the emergency and, thirdly, the need for national coordination due to the effects or nature of the risk. This is what constitutional jurisprudence has defined. The draft law sets out the principles that will govern all actions of the national system in this field: collaboration, cooperation, coordination, inter-territorial solidarity, subsidiarity, efficiency and participation, which are key points of reference for their logical structuring. The bill also sets out the priorities for public authorities to act in the protection of citizens, such as channelling their direct participation or through organizations, enabling them to be informed about the risks that may affect them, how to prevent them and protect themselves and, if the emergency occurs, to be assisted and assisted within the framework of the legal system and the availability of existing means. I would also like to stress, ladies and gentlemen, that article 3 of the draft law defines the National Civil Protection System as an integrator of the civil protection activity of all public administrations, with full respect for their competences in this field, in order to ensure a coordinated and efficient response. The strategy of the National Civil Protection System is regulated and, in particular, the actions of the national system are concrete. These actions focus on anticipation, risk prevention, planning, immediate response to emergencies, recovery - I will say something about this later - system evaluation and inspection, and also on emergencies of national interest. Ladies and gentlemen, I also think it is very important to highlight - and I am sure you will share with me the importance of this incorporation into the draft law - the creation of an Emergency Prevention Fund, managed by the Ministry of the Interior, which is provided from the credits that are recorded annually to the effect in the Law on General Budgets of the State to finance, in the field of the General Administration of the State, preventive activities. Precisely because today we have presented for its eventual validation the royal decree-law for the floods and other natural disasters produced - as you know - since the beginning of the year, I want to clarify that these are reparation measures; they are credits that are financed from the Contingency Fund. Here we are talking about an Emergency Prevention Fund, with the aim of developing preventive activities, such as hazard analysis, risk maps for civil protection, awareness-raising programmes and preventive information for citizens or education in schools. At the same time, ladies and gentlemen, I would also like to point out to you that the National Civil Protection Alert Network, managed by the Ministry of the Interior through the National Centre for Emergency Monitoring and Coordination in this area, is regulated as a system for communicating emergency warnings to the authorities so that essential public services and citizens are informed of a situation such as the one that motivates the activation of the network. In the area of recovery, I would like to point out that for the adoption of recovery measures - that would be the equivalent of what we have done this morning with the presentation of this royal decree-law - the Council of Ministers, on a joint proposal from the Ministers of Finance and Public Administrations and the Interior, and requested, in any case, by the public administrations concerned, is expected to agree to declare an area seriously affected by an emergency of civil protection, and to determine, through the adoption of that agreement, what measures are applicable within those laid down in the draft law that I am currently presenting to your gentlemen. All this I would like to clarify without prejudice to those who expressly require a reservation of law, such as exemptions and bonuses in both fiscal and social security matters. To understand and say in another way - if I may say so - in the face of the usual procedure that we have been using so far, of a royal decree-law which contemplates all the measures necessary to deal with the damage caused by natural or other events, what is foreseen from now on, if the draft law is approved, is that, at the request of the competent public administration and with the agreement of the Ministry of Finance and the Ministry of the Interior, within the scope of this draft law, by the agreement of the Council of Ministers, it is already determined which aid is intended to alleviate the damage that has occurred in the administration or public administrations concerned and that, in turn, they have had to request such a declaration; so that the mechanism is much faster. I insist, with the exception - as I have pointed out - of those aids which normally - as we have presented today - are adopted and which affect tax subsidies or social security. In that case, it cannot be an agreement of the Council of Ministers on the basis of this bill - in its case law - because they have, I insist, express legal reservation. As far as emergencies of national interest are concerned, I would like to point out that they are strictly the case-law of the Constitutional Court. In such a way that emergencies of national interest, and as expressly stated in the law: One, those that require for the protection of persons and property the application of Organic Law 4/1981, which is the regulator of states of alarm, exception and site. Two, those in which it is necessary to provide for the coordination of various public administrations because they affect several autonomous communities and also require a provision of resources at a supra-autonomic level. And three, which, because of their actual or foreseeable dimensions, require national leadership. The most emblematic case which, as you know, the case law of the Constitutional Court is nuclear disasters. I now refer, because it seems absolutely fair and necessary to me, to the human resources of the National Civil Protection System, to which Title III of the draft law is devoted. I would like to begin by saying that the draft law contains in a very special way that the public authorities will promote the training and development of the technical competence of the staff in this field. To this end, the draft law underlines the importance of the training and the work of the National School of Civil Protection, regulating its functions for the first time legally, including the training of staff in this field in the General Administration of the State and other public and private institutions, in collaboration with the civil protection training centres that may have other public administrations and always - I insist - under the principle of cooperation and collaboration. Title IV refers to some instruments of the system. I would like to highlight, for obvious reasons, the Military Emergency Unit, whose intervention will be requested by the Minister of the Interior and ordered by the Minister of Defence, as well as the participation of the State Security Forces and Bodies in civil protection actions. I am now finishing, ladies and gentlemen, by referring to the fact that our relationship with the European Union Civil Protection Mechanism is also reflected in the draft law. It should be recalled that in 1985, when the current law was passed, we had not joined the then European Economic Community, nor had that mechanism been created. Today it is already necessary to bring to the legal framework what is actually being achieved, but without adequate legal coverage. The title therefore highlights the regulation of this Civil Protection Mechanism for the European Union, both to provide our means when they are needed and to receive those from other countries, if they are needed and requested. This gives our National Civil Protection System, if I may, an extra capacity, such as training for our resources when they leave our borders and the security of international support if needed. All that is worthy of consideration and, therefore, I highlight it at this time. I conclude, Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, by summarising the main developments contained in the draft. First, as I pointed out at the beginning of my intervention, there is a move from the current Civil Protection Act to a law of the National Civil Protection System, a law that defines the civil protection policy to the design of the system for carrying out that policy. Secondly, civil protection is defined for the first time, in accordance with constitutional jurisprudence. Thirdly, the strategy of the system is regulated. Fourthly, existing instruments that were not regulated as the National Information Network, the National Alert Network, are regulated and, as noted above, the Risk Prevention Fund is established. Fifthly, emergencies of national interest are regulated by law and the competences of the General Administration of the State in such cases of national emergency are precisely established. Finally, but not least, the participation and training of volunteering is highlighted. Before concluding, ladies and gentlemen, there are around 25,000 civil protection volunteers in Spain, in a strict sense, integrated into some 2,000 groups and associations dedicated to this work. I have wanted to emphasize that we speak strictly of approximately 25,000 Civil Protection volunteers, because if we speak in a broad sense there are other very significant volunteers, such as the members of the Remer, the Emergency Radio Network, about 4,000, as well as other entities and associations, which I do not need to gloss in the rostrum for obvious reasons, such as the Red Cross or various NGOs with a very large number of volunteers involved in these situations. In short, ladies and gentlemen, this project represents an important step forward in the systematisation of a subject as sensitive and as topical for citizens as it is for civil protection and which will enable our country, if approved, to be equipped with one of the best orderly and most advanced civil protection systems of today. Thank you very much, Madam President. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u180	Thank you, Mr. Olabarría. For the Group of the Plural Left, Mr Sixto.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u181	Thank you, Madam President. The draft law of the National Civil Protection System does not represent any qualitative leap from the current law. It is a project that focuses more on security than on coordination, which is the basis of civil protection, leaving aside aspects that today are considered fundamental and that are already being incorporated into the civil protection regulations of many countries in our environment, including France, Portugal or Italy. On the other hand, if Law 2/1985, on Civil Protection, intended to adapt the normative framework for civil protection to the then new autonomous State, as reflected in the explanatory statement, this bill, with amendment to the whole by our parliamentary group, means a re-centralisation, invading autonomous and municipal competences, as the two spokesmen who have already spoken before me have already said. Therefore, we are dealing with a bill that does not represent a substantial change in the civil protection system or a qualitative improvement of the civil protection system, nor is it useful to respond to new needs in this area, nor does it improve the coordination instruments between the State departments themselves that are currently involved in civil protection actions, but shows the lack of respect that the current Government has for the powers of the autonomous communities and the municipalities. These would be some of the issues that reflect the absence of coordination mechanisms, as well as the inclusion of relevant aspects that could improve the system. Currently, civil protection powers are shared in five different ministries and in the autonomous communities, which also have their own security and civil protection departments. The Civil Protection Act includes the Presidency of the Government; the Ministry of Public Administration; the Ministry of Defence, from which the emergency military units come; and the Ministry of Development, on the issue of maritime security. Such an atomized state structure causes civil protection actions to be doubled in many cases and diluted in competitive chaos. This bill not only does not pose a new model, but it does not even resolve this competitive chaos from the point of view of State jurisdiction. In addition, in order to carry out the functions of Civil Protection, it is necessary to coordinate with several technical bodies of the General Administration of the State, which have their competences defined in the risk analysis but which carry it out from an perspective that cannot exactly coincide with the needs of civil protection. With regard to the content of the draft law, it was not understood how the definitions adopted internationally within the strategy for disaster risk reduction had not been used in the most common terms in risk management; on the contrary, the definitions used were not the most appropriate and were not always used correctly throughout the text. Throughout the text there is no mention of the Emergency Network, which has always been an information network composed of very strong volunteers and very closely linked to Civil Protection, working unselfishly since the beginning of the latter. Nor is there any reference to civil protection volunteers, who form the groups in the municipalities of less than 20,000 inhabitants, nor with a new law of volunteering, with which it would have been interesting to establish some kind of relationship. They say that these volunteers are trained at the National School of Civil Protection, so why are they ignored, Minister? Another point to highlight is the small importance given in this project to the protection of the environment; it concerns only the protection of citizens and their property; in some cases it refers to the protection of critical infrastructures, but there is no mention of environmental protection. This is shocking when, in the latest European and national regulations, the issue of the environment and climate change is recurrent, such as the objective of the Floods Directive. The objective of this directive - I quote - is to create a common framework for assessing and reducing in the European Union the risks of floods to human health, the environment, goods and economic activities. This law, which could bring technological developments for better knowledge and risk management, does not present a single measure related to this issue. It serves as an example of what happens in France with its Law on the Modernization of Civil Security, which in its article 9 states that a decree will lay down the technical rules and standards, allowing for the interoperability of radio communications networks and public service information systems competing in civil security missions. On the other hand, neither are the guidelines set for the future to unify signal codes for the entire national territory in the various risks: dams, tsunamis, nuclear... Nor is anything addressed about the use of social networks, which in the United States have been very useful in recent major emergencies. Emergency management and its relationship with the National Emergency Network includes the development of data models and international standards for their management. Another relevant aspect of the draft law that certainly deserves a positive assessment is the chapter on the Prevention Fund; however, it does not only clarify how it is to be financed but also generates a significant contradiction, since another section of the text says that this law does not generate expenditure. Finally, special attention is paid to the competitive invasion that this reform seeks to achieve. Far from adapting the normative framework to the development of the State of self-government, the draft law aims to discourage those communities that, due to the lack of government activity in this area, have been developing a civil protection policy autonomously. From now on, with the incorporation of the Military Emergency Unit into the evil so-called National Civil Protection System, the State will now have at its disposal an instrument with the capacity to centralize the political command of emergency management from the introduction of the emergency concept of national interest, which develops article 28 of the draft law. In this way, once the emergency of national interest has been declared, the command of the operations will be the responsibility of the State, which will entail a dysfunction in terms of management, since at least in Catalonia all the operating bodies that intervene - firemen, police, health care, civil protection - depend on the Generalitat. In addition, it is, at the very least, questionable that the Military Emergency Unit should assume operational leadership in the event of an emergency of national interest, when the operational leadership should be entrusted to an executive management body of a technical-political and non-military nature, Minister. This bill demonstrates the lack of respect that the current Government of Spain has for the competence of the autonomous communities and the municipalities of this country. The principle of subsidiarity, which is a basic concept in establishing mechanisms for coordination and cooperation between administrations, is essential for applying it to civil protection, given the immediate intervention and from the territorial proximity to any crisis situation. On the other hand, it is evident that there is no exclusive competence in civil protection in some autonomous communities. For example, the Statute of Autonomy of Catalonia, in its article 132 on emergencies and civil protection, points out that the Generalitat has exclusive competence in the field of civil protection, which in any case includes the regulation, planning and implementation of measures relating to emergencies and civil security, as well as the management and coordination of civil protection services, including those relating to the prevention and extinguishment of fire, without prejudice to the powers of local governments in this field, respecting what the State establishes, in the exercise of its powers, in the field of public security. In addition, in cases involving emergencies and civil protection beyond Catalonia, the Generalitat will promote mechanisms for collaboration with other autonomous communities and with the State. The ambiguities in the drafting of this bill and the obvious contradictions with the municipal and autonomous powers run counter to the constitutional doctrine, which defines civil protection as a concurrent competence between the State and the autonomous communities, depending in any case on whether the specific emergency corresponds to the supra-autonomic interest, which the Constitutional Court itself defines with three aspects; first, when the regulation of states of alarm, exception and site comes into play; secondly, when the supra-territorial character of the emergency requires the coordination of elements other than the autonomic ones; and thirdly, when the magnitude of the emergency requires a state leadership. It is clear that the Constitutional Court defends the intervention of the State in the field of civil protection, but narrowing down the reasons for the intervention, while protecting the competences attributed to the autonomous communities and the municipalities. On the other hand, it is surprising that there is no reference to municipal and mayor powers in the area of civil protection, which were already contained in Law 2/1985 on Civil Protection and the Law Regulating the Basis of Local Regime. Similarly, it is surprising that government delegates can urge the Ministry of the Interior to declare an emergency of national interest, disregarding the powers assigned to the autonomous communities and municipalities. On the contrary, in the view of our parliamentary group, the new law of the National Civil Protection System should provide coherence and complementary elements to all autonomous systems; under no circumstances should it entail their relegation to an accessory role, and less considering that in the absence of any action by the State in this area they have developed a powerful civil protection policy. For the reasons set out above, my parliamentary group proposes, Minister, that the text be returned to the Government for a thorough reconsideration in order to put an end to this re-centralizing mood of the State, which is also reflected in this bill, by drawing up a text that really brings all the experience that the State has accumulated over the past thirty years. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

Ladies and gentlemen, I repeat that I rise to the rostrum because it seems to me that it is binding and of parliamentary courtesy that I, from the Government, on whose behalf I have tabled the bill today, respond to these amendments in their entirety. I would first like to refer to an issue which has been cross-cutting, with greater or lesser intensity, to the four amendments, and which is the alleged invasion of autonomous powers in the area of civil protection and emergency management and, in line with this, also the existence of an alleged or supposed re-centralizing vocation on the part of the Government and in this case by the Ministry of the Interior. As I have read - and it is my duty, of course - the texts which you have tabled in your amendments as a whole, I bring some questions prepared because it was logical to expect you to defend, with some or other arguments, what you have presented in the written texts of those amendments. First, all the amendments, I insist, with more or less intensity and with more or less competence, have expressed that the draft law violates the autonomous powers of civil protection by claiming the precepts they have in their respective statutes of autonomy. In particular, Mr Guillaumes referred to Article 132 of the Statute of Autonomy of Catalonia, which confers exclusive competence on the autonomous community. Other amendments, as I say, without being as precise as the article of the statute, such as Mr Olabaría or Mrs Jordà, have referred to this or, in general, the representative of the Plural Left, Mr Sixto. I would like to remind you, ladies and gentlemen, that the equivocal character of Article 149 of the Constitution itself and, by mimicking it, of the statutes of autonomy of the first or second generation has the exclusive term, preached in much of its competences. And this has been emphasized by the Constitutional Court in many judgments when it declared that the exclusive adjective is used both in the text of the Constitution and in the text of the statutes of autonomy with a markedly misleading meaning; it has been said repeatedly by the Constitutional Court. It is also sufficient simply to read this constitutional precept or statutory precepts which qualify as exclusive certain powers to warn that exclusivity is without prejudice to the powers of the State or, where appropriate, the autonomous communities in the matter in question. In terms of the Constitutional Court, the scope of exclusivity differs considerably depending on the subject matter in question. As is easy to say, ladies and gentlemen, the assertion of the exclusive nature of a State competence over a matter, without prejudice to the fact that the autonomous communities are responsible for the same subject, is in fact detrimental to the subject of the allegedly exclusive competence. And if I may have a political reference that many of us here know, the Constitution is without prejudice and that is because of the work of a constitutional rapporteur, precisely Mr Miquel Roca - and I say this with all respect and with all thanks to his work - because thanks to those without prejudice the Constitution could be approved with consensus and from the conviction that after that the constituted power and the Constitutional Court would delimit their respective powers. Therefore, let us not use the exclusivity contained in the statutes because that is cheating on the solitary, so to speak, if I may, in colloquial terms. Let me also remind you that we must bear in mind that the technique of exclusivity, understood in its strict sense - that is, as a attribution to a single body, whether state or autonomous, of all public functions on a given matter or sector - is a technique that can be considered exceptional in our general system of competition distribution, and this statement is applicable in a matter such as the one we are talking about: civil protection. Or does anyone think - speaking no longer only from a legal or political point of view but also from a real point of view - what can happen when we are faced with a national emergency or with an emergency that is initially strictly autonomous but that by its very nature evolves and then becomes an emergency of national interest? To some who have spoken from parliamentary groups in Catalonia I can remind you, for example, how in the dramatic forest fires that occurred in July 2012 in Gironinian regions - I experienced that when I was preparing the plan for the evacuation of Figueras, for example - it cost to ask for the participation of the Military Emergency Unit. As I said, I lived it because I was there, at the advanced post of command - I came from Nicosia, where I was participating in the informal European Council at the beginning of the Cyprus pro tempore Presidency in the European Union - and I saw the mayors of the area sighing relieved when they saw the military emergency unit, which provided their citizens and themselves with peace and security. Obviously, the Military Emergency Unit was not activated until the established protocol was implemented, through the Government Delegation in Catalonia and at the request of the Government of Catalonia. Such an instrument, the Emergency Military Unit, was not launched, which is essential in dealing with the disasters or emergency situations that are created in our country. Right now we are seeing it on the occasion of the floods that occurred in early March, especially in the area of the province of Zaragoza, where 450 troops, human resources of the Military Emergency Unit and countless material resources have participated. We have seen how they have contributed to saving people and goods in an emergency. Let's talk about what we're talking about. Mr Guillaumes, do you believe, for example, that if a plan is made for the eventual occurrence of a tsunami in a certain area of Spain, we will limit it to the strict territory of that province or of that autonomous community, or on the contrary we must foresee the consequences that can happen, with feet on the ground and not thinking about other things? Then, obviously, depending on how events evolve, the legal system - for that we design a National Civil Protection System - will tell us at every moment who should intervene, which body or public authority has the competence, who coordinates, etc. But we need to have such contingency protection plans designed because, if they do occur, obviously the citizens who see their people or their assets at risk will not come to say that we have no competence or that the competence is of the other or is of the other. I ask, please, for a bit of wisdom when we are talking about this issue, in which we do not intend at all - not at all - to invade the competences of anyone, but, on the contrary, to structure, to vertebrate a national civil protection system. We are talking precisely about a national system because there are public authorities, such as the autonomous communities, which have very precise competences in the field of civil protection, but the State also has its own, and when the size of the emergency is temporary or of other characteristics and demands it and becomes an emergency of national interest, we must be able to vertebrate all the means and all the capacities that each of the administrations and public authorities have. In short, ladies and gentlemen, this is not a re-centralizing vocation, nor is it a question of establishing prejudices in relation to a matter which, I say to you sincerely, is not there anywhere. In addition, I must say to you with all due respect but with all clarity that it can hardly be held from this rostrum that this project invades competences attributed to the Autonomous Communities when the text that I have presented today has been widely discussed and agreed with the Autonomous Communities within the framework of the National Commission for Civil Protection for a year and a half; ladies and gentlemen, for a year and a half. Perhaps those who have to coordinate internally are some of you. For a year and a half the National Civil Protection Commission has been discussing this project and many observations have been accepted by the Autonomous Communities. The collaboration and coordination between the State administration and that of the Autonomous Communities has been very fluid and the project that I present to you today is the result of the understanding, I insist, between the different administrations involved in the scope of the National Commission for Civil Protection and through other channels that - I repeat - have been operating throughout the year and a half from the Government, from the Ministry of the Interior with the relevant interlocutors of the Autonomous Communities. Therefore, far from being drawn up behind the backs of the autonomous communities, the text incorporates, as I have told you, many of the observations and very correct - that is why we have incorporated them, it would be more lacking! - that have been formulated within the National Civil Protection Commission very qualified representatives of the autonomous communities with competence in this matter, which are almost all of them. As you know, the members of the autonomous communities whose work is in addition to the preparatory work previously carried out by the technicians in this field are precisely represented in the National Commission for Civil Protection. There is - I repeat, and I dare to support it with absolute roundness once again - only one aspect of this bill that alters the competence of the autonomous communities in the area of civil protection. We scrupulously respect the jurisprudence of the Constitutional Court, and from this point of view, even if it is only briefly, I cannot but refer to the fact that it was precisely this jurisprudence of the Constitutional Court that has been outlining the concept and the division, the jurisdictional delimitation in this field over time, with particular reference to two judgments: one in 1984, the 123, and the subsequent one in 1991. The High Court recognizes the State's regulatory competence, providing and implementing coordination techniques - Mr Olabarria, the High Court - and executive competence, taking over management tasks in all emergencies of national interest. That is where the coordinating competence is established, Your Honor. It is logical that the State should have a prior system and an already organized structure and means for the event of a national emergency, since otherwise it is difficult to improvise a response to a threat, to a risk of such characteristics. The present draft law - I affirm once again with all clarity - does not enable the State at all - ex article 149 of the Constitution - as has been erroneously stated, to assume as exclusive competence the executive direction of coordination and unity of command in emergencies that are not declared of national interest. It could only do so when, due to its foreseeable evolution, it requires national coordination due to the effects or nature of the risk, and this is done after formal declaration, in any case, of that emergency as of national interest. I have already referred to the centralizing and homogenizing vision that supposedly underlies the bill. I must simply reiterate that civil protection, ladies and gentlemen, is a sector in which administrative and political action requires the involvement of all the administrations concerned, with all the resources available, in order to achieve the common objective of great importance, which is to respond - I insist - to this situation of threat and emergency which has occurred. Constitutional jurisprudence precisely attributes to the State the normative power to set up a system that could be called basic civil protection at the national level. In other words, if the national system were not so set up, the State would really be deprived of the power to exercise a jurisdiction that the Constitutional Court has repeatedly recognized. What some of you have described as a centralizing project and in your extensive and homogenising written amendments is not the configuration of a basic civil protection system based on coordination, cooperation and mutual respect for the competences of each public administration. I must say that a model similar to that established by the European Civil Protection Mechanism with regard to the Member States of the European Union, managed by an emergency communication and information system with which national networks could be connected in the future, should it be appropriate, is being followed with regard to networks. The Group of the Plural Left has stressed that this project does not represent a qualitative leap with respect to the current law. By time saving - I think I answered you in the presentation of the bill - I just want to remind you in any case that in what is concrete this qualitative leap, in my opinion, obviously respecting yours, of course, it is in consolidating the National Civil Protection System that the current law tried to put into operation with a tool that is the national system. It could not be regulated because it did not exist in 1985 and now, thirty years later, it does exist but needs its regulation to be operational. Mr Sixto has also said in defence of the whole amendment that we do not refer to civil protection volunteers. The truth is that the Republican Left Group and the Plural Left Group have antithetical concepts about this, because while the Republican Left Group says in its amendment that the Government leaves all civil protection in the hands of the volunteers, Mr Sixto from the Plural Left says the opposite. I think that placing us in the middle of these two positions, of the Plural Left and of the Republican Left, in this matter is surely the virtue of this bill. [[Rumors]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u207	Thank you, Mr. President. I am speaking briefly on behalf of the Socialist Parliamentary Group to establish a position in this full debate on the draft law of the National Civil Protection System. The four amendments to the whole recorded by the parliamentary groups are essentially based on competitive reasons. The Socialist Parliamentary Group notes in the context of the existing legal regime and, above all, the case law of the Constitutional Court mentioned repeatedly this afternoon - mainly in judgments 123/1984 and 133/1990 - the existence of State-owned powers in the field of civil protection based on the concepts of public security, supra-territorial disasters and national interests. But there are also the powers of the autonomous communities that have assumed civil protection as exclusive competence in their statutes of autonomy. Therefore, we are faced with a clear assumption - and, moreover, it cannot be otherwise - of competing competences. It expressly states in judgment 123/1984 that in the specific field of civil protection there are competing competences, the distribution of which needs to be designed. In our view, this law is the opportunity to make that design necessary for the distribution of civil protection competences. Ladies and gentlemen, more consensus, more dialogue and less fighting. However, this certainty as to competences does not prevent us from appreciating - and many experts and the autonomous communities with whom we have spoken - that there is a certain re-centralizing vocation in this draft law that the Government has referred to the General Courts, such as the fact that a body of political cooperation between administrations has not been formed in the style of a sectoral conference, something that does happen in many other competences that are transferred to the autonomous communities, such as health or education, or even in competing competences, such as that of culture. We have also noted that the functions of the authorities of the General Administration of the State in the field of civil protection have been strengthened and, perhaps, the autonomous powers in this area have not been well defined. In short, it is not - and less so on the part of my group - to place the emphasis on tightening the rope from one end or another to assert the state powers by denying the autonomous, or vice versa; it is to make an effort to place the emphasis on the need for cooperation between all administrations. The need for cooperation and proper coordination of the exercise of their functions by the competent administrations and even with organisations and citizens is not visible in any matter such as this; we must therefore make a consensus effort to protect the safety of citizens. However, these assessments are not enough for us to vote in favour of the amendments as a whole that we are debating today. We believe that the Civil Protection Act of 1985 should be updated by recognizing new and important aspects, such as the Emergency Military Unit regime, the Prevention Fund, the natural disaster aid system and some other equivalents. Having said that, the Socialist Group is also aware that the bill has other deficits, and I will mention a few very briefly. For example, we do not understand very well why the Government and the People's Party have started the house on the roof in a law of this importance, which is intimately linked to other legal changes that seem to be not going to be dealt with or are going to be done outside this law. Its objective is to establish a national civil protection system to ensure the coordination, cohesion and effectiveness of public policies in this area. Its ultimate goal is nothing other than to be able to address in the most rapid and effective manner the emergency situations that may occur for the benefit of those affected, and to this end the six phases or actions of the National Civil Protection System are defined and regulated - anticipation, prevention, planning, immediate response, recovery and evaluation and monitoring of the system for effective operation - and new instruments are created to improve the system, such as the national civil protection information network, the national civil protection alert network or the emergency prevention fund. In addition, the powers of the organs of the General Administration of the State are established, in which the Ministry of the Interior of course prevails, although it is true that it is not the only department involved: the role of the delegations of the Government, the State Security Forces and Corpses and the Emergency Military Unit is also recognized. This, Mr Sixto, is due to the cross-cutting nature of civil protection. [[Rumors]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-03-26-CD150326.u213	The Government and the Ministry of the Interior have been working on this bill since the beginning of the legislature. Many issues raised by the various autonomous communities within the National Civil Protection Commission and by the Spanish Federation of Municipalities and Provinces have been incorporated into the local administration commission. An important effort has been made to achieve maximum consensus, and it has been achieved, with no technical difference remaining with respect to the essentials. This fact, the fact that no councillor from any autonomous community has expressed essential technical reservations, together with the fact that they have justified their amendments with the same usual rhetoric and without concrete action, is the only thing that makes us think that they are artificial amendments, since they have no basis. The main criticism has been the competitive invasion. In this respect, I would like to say, as the Minister has explained, that the draft law scrupulously respects the broad autonomous competence in this area. As has been said, the Constitutional Court is the one that, through two judgments, has outlined the division of jurisdiction, a concurrent competence where the State has competence in emergencies of national interest, specifically in three cases: when a state of alarm, exception and site is declared, when emergencies affect several autonomous communities or when emergencies, by their size, require a national leadership. The competence of the State in this area has its limits, as do the powers of the autonomous communities, since civil protection is a public security policy. The State therefore has regulatory and executive powers. It is logical that the State should be prepared to deal with a national emergency situation and, in order to do so, it should first have a comprehensive system, structure and adequate means. Article 3.2 of the draft law sets out, in my view, the principles that should govern the actions of the civil protection system: collaboration, cooperation, coordination, inter-territorial solidarity, subsidiarity, efficiency and participation. I could expand further, but I am not going to do so because the minister has already explained the bill very well and has already responded to the various questions raised. However, I would not like to end without thanking the Socialist Group for its position and willingness to reach agreements and contribute to improving this law. I would like to reiterate the willingness of my parliamentary group and the Government hand in hand to reach agreements. We are ambitious about reaching consensus on this issue, and we would like to see a text supported by most of the parliamentary groups. That is why I would ask the groups which have tabled amendments in their entirety to ask for two things: on the one hand, that their contributions be converted into partial amendments and, on the other hand, that, given the subject covered by this bill, they have common sense or, as is said in our land, Mr Sixto, trellat; common sense. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
